(157)

With this definition, it is possible to embed all the deformations of the theory into
\mathrm{SO}(3,3)
representations with the following mapping

f_{ABC}=\mathchoice{\big(X_{mn}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.82854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{rs}}}_{{{pq}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.64365pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\big(X_{mn}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.82854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{rs}}}_{{{pq}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.64365pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\big(X_{mn}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.82854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{rs}}}_{{{pq}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.64365pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\big(X_{mn}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.03868pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.82854pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44894pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{rs}}}_{{{pq}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.70258pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.64365pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3169pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,[\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}]^{mn}\,[\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}]^{pq}\,[\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8526pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.75186pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{C}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8526pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.75186pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{C}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8526pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.75186pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{C}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.22598pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8526pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.75186pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{C}}}}]_{rs}\;,

(158)

\xi_{A}=\xi_{mn}\,[\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}]^{mn}\;,

(159)

while the parameter
\theta
is a scalar in both representations.

The expression of the scalar potential in the
\mathrm{SL}(4)
formalism is

\begin{split}V=&\,\frac{1}{64}\bigg(\theta^{2}\,X^{8}+\frac{1}{4}Q_{mn}\,Q_{pq}\,X^{-2}\big(2M^{mp}M^{nq}-M^{mn}M^{pq}\big)+\\
&+\frac{3}{2}\xi_{mn}\,\xi_{pq}\,X^{-2}\,M^{mp}M^{nq}+\frac{1}{4}\,\tilde{Q}^{mn}\tilde{Q}^{pq}\,X^{-2}\big(2M_{mp}M_{nq}-M_{mn}M_{pq}\big)+\\
&-\theta\,X^{3}\big(Q_{mn}M^{mn}-\tilde{Q}^{mn}M_{mn}\big)+Q_{mn}\tilde{Q}^{mn}X^{-2}\bigg)\;,\end{split}

(160)

where the matrix
M^{mn}
is the inverse of the coset representative
M_{mn}
. In the
\mathrm{SO}(3,3)
formalism, and setting
\xi_{A}=0
, it becomes

\begin{split}V=&\;\frac{1}{64}\bigg[\frac{X^{2}}{2}f_{ABC}f_{DEF}\Big(\frac{1}{3}M^{AD}M^{BE}M^{CF}+\Big(\frac{2}{3}\eta^{AD}-M^{AD}\Big)\eta^{BE}\eta^{CF}\Big)+\\
&+\theta^{2}X^{-8}-\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}\theta X^{-3}f_{ABC}M^{ABC}\bigg]\;,\end{split}

(161)

Appendix B
Massive IIA supergravity in the democratic formulation

In this Appendix, we provide some details about type IIA supergravity in
10
dimensions in presence of Romans’ mass
68
, usually referred to as
massive type IIA supergravity
. This theory admits a democratic formulation
17
, which we adopt in Section
5
. Its bosonic fields consist of the NS-NS fields
\{G,B_{(2)},\Phi\}
, plus the Ramond-Ramond degrees of freedom, which are doubled with respect to the standard formulation
\{C_{2p+1}\}_{p=0,1,2,3,4}
. The equations of motion for the bosonic sector can be obtained by varying the following pseudoaction:

S_{\textup{II A}}^{\textup{democratic}}=\frac{1}{2\kappa_{10}^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{10}x\ \sqrt{-G}\Bigl(e^{-2\Phi}\bigl(\mathcal{R}+4(\partial\Phi)^{2}-\frac{1}{12}|H_{(3)}|^{2}\bigr)-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{p=0}^{5}\frac{|F_{(2p)}|^{2}}{(2p)!}\Bigr)\,,

(162)

where we have defined the field strengths

H_{(3)}=\differential B_{2}\,,\qquad\quad F=\differential C-\differential B\wedge C+F_{(0)}\,e^{B}\;.

(163)

The following duality relations are needed in order to restore the correct counting of the degrees of freedom in the R-R sector

F_{(0)}=\star F_{(10)}\;,\qquad F_{(2)}=-\star F_{(8)}\;,\qquad F_{(4)}=\star F_{(6)}\;,\qquad H_{(7)}=e^{-2\Phi}\star H_{(3)}\,.

(164)

By definition, the field strengths (
163
) obey the following modified Bianchi identities

\displaystyle\differential H_{(3)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential H_{(7)}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p=0}^{4}F_{(2p)}\wedge\star F_{(2p+2)}=0\quad,

(165)

\displaystyle\differential F_{(0)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential F_{(2)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(0)}=0\quad,

(166)

\displaystyle\differential F_{(4)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(2)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential F_{(6)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(4)}=0\quad,

(167)

\displaystyle\differential F_{(8)}-H_{(3)}\wedge F_{(6)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential F_{(10)}=0\;.

(168)

Making use of the duality relations (
164
), we can map the Bianchi identities (
165
), (
166
), (
167
), (
168
) to the equations of motion for the dual fields, either in the NS-NS or the R-R sector

\displaystyle\differential\bigl(e^{2\Phi}\star H_{(7)}\bigr)=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\bigl(e^{-2\Phi}\star H_{(3)}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p=0}^{4}F_{(2p)}\wedge\star F_{(2p+2)}=0\quad,

(169)

\displaystyle\differential\star F_{(10)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\star F_{(8)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(10)}=0\quad,

(170)

\displaystyle\differential\star F_{(6)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(8)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\star F_{(4)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(6)}=0\quad,

(171)

\displaystyle\differential\star F_{(2)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(4)}=0\quad,

\displaystyle\qquad\differential\star F_{(0)}=0\quad.

(172)

In particular, the equations of motion in the R-R sector can be formally written as

\differential\star F_{(2p)}+H_{(3)}\wedge\star F_{(2p+2)}=0\;.

(173)

As for the remaining fields, the dilaton has the following equation of motion

\nabla_{M}\nabla^{M}\Phi-\nabla_{M}\Phi\nabla^{M}\Phi+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{R}-\frac{1}{8\times 3!}|H_{(3)}|^{2}=0\quad,

(174)

while the Einstein’s equations are

e^{-2\Phi}\big(\mathcal{R}_{MN}+2\nabla_{M}\nabla_{N}\Phi-\frac{1}{4}H_{MPQ}{H_{N}}^{PQ}\big)-\frac{1}{2}(F_{(2)}^{2})_{MN}-\frac{1}{2\times 3!}(F_{(4)}^{2})_{MN}+\\
+\frac{1}{4}G_{MN}\big(|F_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2!}|F_{(2)}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4!}|F_{(4)}|^{2}\big)=0\quad.

(175)

Appendix C
Non-abelian D-brane effective action

Due to the non-Abelianity of the fields involved, the effective action of a stack of coincident D
p
-branes gets modifications with respect to the action for single branes.
In this Section, we describe the key features of this action
63
;
62
, following the notation adopted in
26
,
12
. The action splits into two parts: the Dirac-Born-Infeld (DBI) and Wess-Zumino (WZ) actions. Written in the string frame, they take the form:

S^{\textup{DBI}}_{\textrm{D}p}=-T_{\textrm{D}p}\int_{\textup{WV}(\textrm{D}p)}\differential^{p+1}\xi\;\Tr\bigg( e^{-\hat{\Phi}} \sqrt{-\det(\mathbb{M}_{\mu\nu}) \det(\tensor{\mathbb{Q}}{^i_j})} \; \bigg)\quad,

(176)

S^{\textup{WZ}}_{\textrm{D}p}=\mu_{\textrm{D}p}\int_{\textup{WV}(\textrm{D}p)}\Tr\{\mathrm{P}\bigg[e^{i\lambda\iota_{Y}\iota_{Y}}\Big(\boldsymbol{\hat{C}}\wedge e^{\hat{B}_{(2)}}\Big)\wedge e^{\lambda\mathcal{F}}\bigg]\bigg\}\quad,

(177)

where we limit our attention to the bosonic part of the action.
The matrices
\mathbb{M}_{\mu\nu}
and
\mathchoice{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}
appearing in the DBI action are

\mathbb{M}_{\mu\nu}=\mathrm{P}\Big[\hat{E}_{\mu\nu}+\hat{E}_{\mu i}(\mathbb{Q}^{-1}-\delta)^{ij}\hat{E}_{j\nu}\Big]+\lambda\mathcal{F}_{\mu\nu}\quad,

(178)

\mathchoice{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\mathbb{Q}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}=\mathchoice{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}+i\lambda[Y^{i},Y^{k}]\hat{E}_{kj}\quad,

(179)

where

\hat{E}_{MN}=\hat{g}_{MN}+\hat{B}_{MN}\quad.

(180)

In the expression (
177
) for the WZ action,
\boldsymbol{C}
stands for the polyform corresponding to the sum of all the
C_{(p)}
fields,
\iota_{Y}
is the interior product by the
Y^{i}
vector and
\mathcal{F}
is the non-Abelian field-strength of the gauge field
\mathcal{A}
of the super Yang-Mills theory along the branes:

\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{d}\mathcal{A}+i\mathcal{A}\wedge\mathcal{A}\;.

(181)

The position of the
Dp
-branes in the transverse space is encoded in
y^{i}=\lambda Y^{Ii}t_{I}
, where
t_{I}
are the generators of the Yang-Mills gauge algebra, such that

[t_{I},t_{J}]=-i{g_{IJ}}^{K}t_{K}\quad,\quad\mathrm{Tr}[t_{I}t_{J}]=N_{Dp}\,\delta_{IJ}\quad.

(182)

N_{Dp}
is the number of branes, not to be confused with the dimension of the gauge algebra
\mathfrak{N}

50
;
51
;
15
;
12
(the two quantities only coincide in the Abelian case). The constant
\lambda
is related to the string length according to
\lambda=2\pi{\ell_{S}}^{2}
.

\mathrm{P}[-]
is the pullback of the bulk fields over the
D6
-brane worldvolume. For instance, the pullback of
\hat{E}_{\mu\nu}
is

\mathrm{P}[\hat{E}_{\mu\nu}]=\hat{E}_{\mu\nu}+\lambda D_{\mu}Y^{i}\hat{E}_{i\mu}+\lambda D_{\nu}Y^{i}\hat{E}_{\mu i}+\lambda^{2}D_{\mu}Y^{i}D_{\nu}Y^{j}\hat{E}_{ij}\;.

(183)

Here, the covariant derivative is

D_{\mu}Y^{i}\equiv\partial_{\mu}Y^{i}-i[\mathcal{A}_{\mu},Y^{i}]\;.

(184)

All the hatted fields are evaluated at the position of the
D6
-branes with a Taylor expansion around
y^{i}=0
(static gauge):

\hat{\Phi}(x^{\mu},y^{i})=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}Y^{i_{1}}\cdots Y^{i_{k}}\partial_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial_{i_{k}}\Phi(x^{\mu},y^{i})\Big|_{y^{i}=0}\;.

(185)

The contributions to the bosonic effective action for the orientifold plane in the string frame, instead, are given by

S^{\mathrm{DBI}}_{\mathrm{O}p}=-T_{\mathrm{O}6}\int\mathrm{d}^{7}\xi\ e^{-\Phi}\sqrt{-\mathrm{det}(g_{\mu\nu})}\quad,

(186)

S^{\mathrm{WZ}}_{\mathrm{O}p}=\mu_{\mathrm{O}6}\int_{WV(\mathrm{O}p)}\hat{C}_{(p+1)}\quad.

(187)

Compared to (
176
) and (
177
), no other terms appear, since orientifold planes are not dynamical objects, at least perturbatively.

Appendix D
Local branches of solutions to the HJ equation

In this Appendix, we complement the discussion of Subsection
8.2
with additional details about the perturbative solutions to the HJ equation around the critical points of half-maximal 7D supergravity listed in Table
8
.

When we solve perturbatively around the supersymmetric vacuum
\mathbf{1^{\prime}}
, we obtain

•

at zero order
f^{(0)}=\frac{5}{32}q
,

•

the eight branches of solutions for the superpotential are associated to eight possible solutions to the Hamilton-Jacobi equation at order two, which correspond to the following possibilities for the matrix of second-order coefficients:

f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5(3\pm 1)}{32}q,\,\frac{3(3\pm 7)}{32}q,\,\frac{3(3\pm 7)}{64}q\bigg)\;,

(188)

where the variables in the expansion of the superpotential are the scalar fields of (
107
), i. e.
\{X,T,Y\}
.

The analytic solutions correspond to the following branches

\begin{split}f^{\mathbf{1^{\prime}}a}\;:&\quad f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5}{8}q,\,-\frac{3}{8}q,\,-\frac{3}{16}q\bigg)\;,\\
f^{\mathbf{1^{\prime}}b}\;:&\quad f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5}{8}q,\,\frac{15}{16}q,\,\frac{15}{32}q\bigg)\;.\end{split}

(189)

Concerning the solutions around the non-supersymmetric point
\mathbf{3^{\prime}}
, we have

•

at zero order
f^{(0)}=\frac{5}{2^{1/5}\sqrt{3}\cdot 16}q
,

•

at order two, the eight branches are characterized by

f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5\cdot 2^{1/5}(\sqrt{3}\pm\sqrt{7})}{16}q,\,\frac{9\,(1\pm\sqrt{5})}{16\cdot 2^{1/5}\sqrt{3}}q,\,\frac{3\sqrt{3}(1\pm\sqrt{5})}{32\cdot 2^{1/5}}q\bigg)\;.

(190)

In this case, the analytic solutions correspond to the following branches

\begin{split}f^{\mathbf{3^{\prime}}a}\;:&\quad f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5\cdot 2^{1/5}(\sqrt{3}-\sqrt{7})}{16}q,\,\frac{9\,(1-\sqrt{5})}{16\cdot 2^{1/5}\sqrt{3}}q,\,\frac{3\sqrt{3}(1-\sqrt{5})}{32\cdot 2^{1/5}}q\bigg)\;,\\
f^{\mathbf{3^{\prime}}b}\;:&\quad f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5\cdot 2^{1/5}(\sqrt{3}+\sqrt{7})}{16}q,\,\frac{9\,(1+\sqrt{5})}{16\cdot 2^{1/5}\sqrt{3}}q,\,\frac{3\sqrt{3}(1+\sqrt{5})}{32\cdot 2^{1/5}}q\bigg)\;.\end{split}

(191)

The solutions around the supersymmetric point
\mathbf{1}
feature

•

at zero order
f^{(0)}=\frac{5}{16\cdot 2^{3/5}\cdot 3^{2/5}}q
,

•

at order two

f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5\,(3\pm 1)}{32}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)^{3/5}q,\,\frac{9\,(3\pm 7)}{32\cdot 2^{3/5}\cdot 3^{2/5}}q,\,\frac{3\pm 1}{32}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)^{2/5}q\bigg)\;.

(192)

The analytic solutions correspond to the following branches

\begin{split}f^{\mathbf{1}a}\;:&\quad f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5}{8}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)^{3/5}q,\,-\frac{9}{8\cdot 2^{3/5}\cdot 3^{2/5}}q,\,\frac{1}{8}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)^{2/5}q\bigg)\;,\\
f^{\mathbf{1}b}\;:&\quad f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(\frac{5}{8}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)^{3/5}q,\,\frac{45}{16\cdot 2^{3/5}\cdot 3^{2/5}}q,\,\frac{1}{8}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)^{2/5}q\bigg)\;.\end{split}

(193)

For completeness, we also report the results of the computation around the vacuum
\mathbf{3}
, which is an unstable solution:

•

at zero order
f^{(0)}=\frac{5\cdot 2^{1/5}}{16\cdot 3^{9/10}}q
,

•

at order two, solving the equations with respect to the matrix of second order derivatives gives eight complex-value solutions:

f^{(2)}=\text{diag}\bigg(-\frac{5\cdot 2^{4/5}(3\pm\sqrt{21})}{16\cdot 3^{11/10}}q,\,-\frac{27\,(1\pm\sqrt{5})}{16\cdot 2^{4/5}\cdot 3^{9/10}}q,\,\frac{2^{1/5}(-3\pm i\sqrt{3})}{16\cdot 3^{9/10}}q\bigg)\;.

(194)

This is expected, due to the violation of the BF bound, as can be seen from equations (
141
) and (
142
). In particular, the instability comes from the scalar field
Y
and the non-real values for the derivatives are found in the corresponding direction.
